## [2026-08-01T12:10:00Z] Task Completed
- Task: Claude independently verified the XP-award diff above, then attempted to add the real concurrent race test Codex couldn't run. First attempt used ThreadPoolExecutor with separate TestClient/session per thread — failed deterministically (`sqlite3.DatabaseError: no more rows available`) because this fixture's engine uses StaticPool, which (verified directly) hands every session the literal same underlying DBAPI connection object; concurrent commits against one shared sqlite3 connection corrupt cursor state regardless of application correctness. A single-threaded manually-interleaved two-session variant was considered and rejected for the same reason — there is only one real transaction underneath both sessions, so it wouldn't model independent concurrent transactions either. Reverted the broken test rather than ship something flaky or misleading; replaced with a code comment documenting why, and confirming the explicitly required test scenarios (double-click, refresh, retry, two tabs, resume-completed — all sequential-request patterns) are already covered by the sequential idempotency tests, with transactional correctness (award_xp-before-commit, IntegrityError caught and re-fetched) confirmed by direct code review.
- Files changed: backend/tests/test_service_desk_attempts.py; tasks/loop-log.md
- Result: pass — full backend suite 289/289 passes, compileall clean. Genuine concurrent-transaction testing would require a file-backed SQLite or Postgres test fixture instead of the current in-memory StaticPool setup; flagged as a test-infrastructure gap, not attempted further in this phase.

## [2026-08-02T05:20:00Z] Task Completed
- Task: Performed real end-to-end browser testing of the Service Desk <-> Nexus integration with both apps running together (Docker Compose staging stack, `nexus-staging`, accessed at the LAN IP per the pre-existing CSP/host quirk). Wrote `frontend/tests/e2e/service-desk-integration.spec.js`, a Playwright spec that drives the actual UI (login, ticket page, Directory tool unlock, resolve/close dialog) rather than calling APIs directly, then verifies Nexus-side authority: attempt exists, grade is server-computed (`passed`, `overall_score`), idempotent `/complete` replay (twice, same key) doesn't change the score, a completely clean second browser context resumes the completed attempt, a second student gets 403 on the first student's attempt, an admin sees the full event timeline including `ticket.close` and `directory.*` events, mentor feedback posts and is visible to the student.
- Found and fixed three real defects, none of which any existing unit test caught because every fixture used hand-written fixture values that didn't match the real backend contract:
  1. **`GET /api/service-desk/assignments`'s `maximum_attempts` is `int | None`** (unlimited attempts is the default) but the service-desk-app's `isAssignment()` type guard required strictly `typeof === 'number'`, silently filtering every real assignment out of `listAssignments()`. Fixed in the other repo (see its own loop-log).
  2. **Every ID field Nexus returns (`assignment.id`, `scenario_id`, `latest_published_version.id`, `attempt.id`, `grade.id`, `grade.attempt_id`, `grade.scenario_version_id`) is a JSON number** (SQLAlchemy integer PKs via `jsonable_encoder`), but the service-desk-app's `NexusAssignment`/`NexusAttempt`/`NexusGrade` interfaces and their runtime type guards declared/required `string`. Combined with defect #1, this meant `listAssignments()`/`getAttempt()`/`completeAttempt()` validation would have always rejected real backend responses — the entire Nexus sync path was silently a no-op end-to-end, masked completely by string-typed test fixtures (`'assignment-1'`, `'attempt-1'`) that never represented reality. Fixed in the other repo.
  3. **`_record_event` in `backend/app/routers/service_desk.py` unconditionally overwrote `attempt.current_state` with the last event's `resulting_state`, regardless of `tool`.** Directory/remote-desktop evidence events carry that tool's own overlay shape (e.g. `{locked, mfaEnrolled}`), not ticket state (`{notes, events, status, closure, ...}`). A directory action recorded after a ticket action corrupted `current_state` with the wrong shape; on resume, the client's `hasNexusTicketState()` check only verifies non-emptiness (not shape) and merges the corrupted state into `ticketOverlays`, crashing the ticket page render (`t.notes is not iterable`) on the very next load. Fixed here: only `tool == "ticket"` events now update `attempt.current_state`/`current_state_hash` (append-only event log and `state_version` counter are unaffected for every tool, preserving order and the audit trail).
- Also found two test-infrastructure issues (not application defects): (a) the backend's CSRF-origin-validation middleware (`app/main.py`) correctly rejects state-changing requests carrying a session cookie unless `Origin` matches a trusted origin — Playwright's `page.request.post()` doesn't set this automatically (unlike in-page `fetch()`), so the spec's direct completion-replay and feedback POSTs needed an explicit `Origin` header; (b) the Directory tool's "Unlock account" control is a two-step confirm dialog (trigger button and the modal's confirm button share the exact same accessible name), matching the same pattern already documented for `ResolveDialog` — the spec now scopes the second click to `getByRole('dialog')`.
- Files changed: backend/app/routers/service_desk.py; backend/tests/test_service_desk_attempts.py (added `test_non_ticket_tool_events_do_not_overwrite_resumable_ticket_state`); frontend/tests/e2e/service-desk-integration.spec.js (new); tasks/loop-log.md
- Result: pass. Backend: `compileall` clean, full suite 297/297 (296 baseline + 1 new), `alembic current` unchanged at `0036_service_desk_mentor_feedback` (no migration needed — Python-only fix). ## [2026-08-02T10:25:00Z] Task Completed
- Task: Phase 7 (Service Desk Learning/Simulation mode polish). User confirmed via question: waive grading penalties in Learning Mode.
- Found and fixed: the Remote Desktop tool's "Learning mode" dropdown (Guided/Practice/Assessment) was fully student-editable with no gating and no connection to the actual Nexus assignment - any student could switch themselves into "Assessment" mode (no hints, feedback withheld) mid-ticket, violating "students should not accidentally enter Simulation Mode before they are ready." `setLearningMode` has exactly one call site (this selector); gated it behind the same `canReviewScenario` (admin/mentor) check already used for `MentorScenarioReview`. Committed separately as e35c134 in the outer repo, verified live in a real browser (standalone/student identity: selector count 0).
- Found and fixed: `compute_grade()` applied hint-use and unresolved-close penalties identically regardless of assignment mode - "Learning Mode" assignments were scored exactly like "Simulation Mode," with no "no punishment for experimentation" behavior. Added `is_learning_mode = attempt.mode == "learning"` gate: both `unresolved_penalty` and `hint_penalty` are waived (forced to 0) when true; `passed`/`resolved`/`technical_complete` are UNCHANGED (still reflect whether the ticket was actually verified-resolved - Learning Mode waives the score deduction, it doesn't redefine what "resolved" means). Added a distinct `feedback_summary` for learning-mode attempts and a new `details.is_learning_mode` field for mentor-review transparency.
- Important discovery while fixing this: `tests/test_service_desk_attempts.py`'s `setup_assignment()` helper hardcoded `mode="learning"` for every existing test - purely incidental (any string satisfied the required field before this change), but it meant the grading-formula tests (`test_two_hints_use_one_free_hint_and_normalize_score`, `test_unresolved_close_applies_penalty_and_fails`, etc.) would have silently started asserting the WRONG expected scores once mode-awareness landed, since real seeded assignments actually use `mode="simulation"` (seed.py). Changed the helper's default to `mode="simulation"` (matching real seed data) with an explicit `mode` override parameter, and added two new tests (`test_learning_mode_waives_hint_penalty`, `test_learning_mode_waives_unresolved_close_penalty`) that explicitly request `mode="learning"` to cover the new waiver behavior. This has zero effect on current production data since every existing seeded assignment is already `mode="simulation"`.
- Files changed: backend/app/services/service_desk_grading.py; backend/tests/test_service_desk_attempts.py; tasks/loop-log.md
- Result: pass. compileall clean; full suite 299/299 (297 baseline + 2 new); alembic current unchanged at 0036_service_desk_mentor_feedback (no migration - pure grading-logic change, no schema change).

## 2026-08-28 00:20 UTC — P1-1 / PR #29: review round 8 (R17, R18)

- R17 (P1): `cp -al || cp -a` venv snapshot could nest itself when
  `$NEXUS_BACKUP_DIR` is on another filesystem (`cp -al` makes a partial dir,

  `backend/.venv`. Fix: `snapshot_tree()` helper that `rm -rf`s the dest before
  each attempt; used for the step-7 snapshot and the `restore_venv` staging copy.

  serving-checkout check, so an accidental run from a worktree overwrote the
  launcher with unreviewed code. Fix: refresh only after step 1 passes, and
  never on `--dry-run`.

  (S19 EXDEV snapshot, S20 dry-run installs nothing, S18 extended). All pass.

## 2026-08-28 02:05 UTC — P1-1 / PR #29: review round 11 (R24, R25, R26)

- R24 (P1): Alembic honoured an ambient `DATABASE_URL` (config loads .env with
  override=False), so the guard/migration could hit a different DB than the
  backup + restart. Fix: `alembic_backend()` exports

  `do_rollback` stops after checkout/venv restore, logs MANUAL INTERVENTION,
  leaves the service down. DEPLOYMENT.md step 1 says keep the pre-swap backup.

  0, and leave stale files. Fix: re-check `git status --porcelain` after the
  step-6 checkout and abort if dirty; rollback checkout warns the same way.
- Tests: `scripts/tests/deploy_failure_sim.sh` now 44 cases / 173 assertions

## [2026-09-03T21:05:00Z] Task Completed
- Task: Group 3 of the approved Service Desk redesign - first-class escalation as a terminal outcome, with server-authoritative grading. Carried directly by the orchestrator because Codex is rate-limited until 2026-09-07.
- Files changed: backend/app/services/service_desk_escalation.py (new); backend/tests/test_service_desk_escalation.py (new); backend/app/services/service_desk_grading.py; backend/app/routers/service_desk.py; backend/app/services/service_desk_workspace_view.py; backend/tests/test_service_desk_workspace_view.py; backend/tests/test_service_desk_attempts.py; service-desk-app/packages/shared/src/escalation.ts (new) + index.ts; service-desk-app/packages/simulation-engine/src/actions/index.ts; service-desk-app/apps/web/components/EscalateDialog.tsx (rewrite) + EscalateDialog.test.tsx (new); service-desk-app/apps/web/components/OutcomeBar.tsx; service-desk-app/apps/web/components/TicketSessionProvider.tsx.
- Behaviour: fixed 7-value escalation reason taxonomy (server + mirrored in shared). New trusted action ticket.escalate is validated at the router boundary (reason in ESCALATION_REASONS, non-empty routeTeam, ticketId), and is trusted only for a scenario whose server-owned EscalationProfile.expected is set, only for the declared route and an accepted reason, and only after investigation and diagnosis are established on the trusted ledger - otherwise it records untrusted and 409s as protected. A trusted ticket.escalate terminates the attempt like ticket.close. compute_grade gains an escalation branch: inc2506 (restricted payroll) - scenario.apply-safe-remediation is prohibited => critical_failure, cannot pass; the correct path is investigate + diagnose + document + escalate to Identity & Access. inc2508 (phishing) - the safe-remediation step is required containment AND escalation to Information Security is required; containment alone or escalation alone does not resolve.
- Correction 2 applied: process credit for an escalation outcome is NORMALIZED across the applicable categories (investigation, diagnosis, documentation, and the remediation weight repurposed as the escalation slot; verification only when the profile requires verifiable containment). Verification is reported as not-applicable (containment_met = null, verification_applicable = false), never as earned work. Full applicable-category performance => normalized 100%, and POINTS_BY_PRIORITY scaling is unchanged. Ordinary scenarios never enter this branch; an ordinary-scenario ticket.escalate is recorded untrusted and does not change the score.
- Deviation from the design doc: pre-completion the workflow rail's fix stage stays mode "fix" and workspace_view.escalation is {available, route} only - the student sees escalation is available and where it routes, but is never told before the outcome that escalation is the correct answer (design doc section 3e and the sprint's anti-leakage instruction take precedence over the "mode: escalate" hint in sections 2a/2c). Full client-side escalation grade preview in the simulation engine is deferred; the server remains authoritative and the dialog gates on authoritative workspace_view stage state.
- Result: backend compile + ruff clean; 221 focused Service Desk / grading / workspace-view / progression / v2 tests pass (11 new in test_service_desk_escalation.py), no new Alembic migration. Two pre-existing generic "every converted scenario passes the remediation workflow" parametrizations now exclude inc2506/inc2508 (their pass/fail is covered by the dedicated escalation tests) - the old assertions encoded the pre-Group-3 behaviour that self-serving those tickets is acceptable. service-desk-app lint 5/5, typecheck 10/10, tests web 97 / shared 37 / simulation-engine 182 / ui 29, web build 44 pages.
- Next: Group 4 (ticket-level debrief).

## [2026-09-03T21:45:00Z] Task Completed
- Task: Group 4 of the approved Service Desk redesign - the post-completion ticket debrief. Orchestrator-implemented (Codex rate-limited).
- Files changed: backend/app/services/service_desk_workspace_view.py (build_debrief + helpers); backend/app/routers/service_desk.py (_debrief_for_attempt, _grade_dict debrief param, get_attempt + complete_attempt wiring); backend/tests/test_service_desk_workspace_view.py; backend/tests/test_service_desk_escalation.py; service-desk-app/apps/web/components/TicketDebrief.tsx (new); service-desk-app/apps/web/components/TicketWorkspace.tsx; service-desk-app/apps/web/components/TicketSessionProvider.tsx; service-desk-app/apps/web/lib/nexus-service-desk-client.ts (NexusDebrief type).
- Behaviour: build_debrief runs only when the attempt is finished (status != in_progress) and is attached to the grade payload on GET /attempts/{id} and POST /complete - never while in progress. It carries authoritative result (passed, score, attempts_remaining, outcome: resolved | escalated | needs_another_try), five process category cards with a status of full / partial / missed / not_applicable plus a re-narration of the ordering signal (evidence recorded after a change could not count as pre-change work), the student's own closure note with three advisory cause/action/verification presence hints that do not affect the score, the authored "stronger troubleshooting path" (escalation scenarios express it as investigate -> diagnose -> [contain] -> escalate to <route> -> document), and an "was escalation appropriate here" yes/no with rationale for every completed ticket. Verification for a pure-escalation outcome shows as not_applicable with 0/0, never as earned work.
- Frontend: TicketWorkspace renders <TicketDebrief> instead of the working surface whenever an authoritative grade exists for the ticket; the provider now restores that grade on reload for failed attempts too (previously only "completed"), so the debrief survives a refresh. "Return to <module>" (validated returnTo) is the primary action.
- Leakage: in-progress GET /attempts/{id} has grade == null and no stronger_path; a dedicated test asserts the debrief only appears post-completion.
- Result: backend ruff clean, 138+ focused Service Desk / workspace-view / grading tests pass (5 new debrief tests); no Alembic migration. service-desk-app lint 5/5, typecheck 10/10, tests web 97 / shared 37 / simulation-engine 182 / ui 29, web build 44 pages.
- Deviation: no dedicated TicketDebrief render test - the repo tests Next-hook components only through pure helpers, and build_debrief has full backend coverage; "survives reload" / "grade shown after completion" are E2E concerns and browser E2E is unavailable in this environment (no credentials).
- Next: Group 5 (light/dark semantic token system, typography, buttons, gamification demotion).

## [2026-09-04T05:00:00Z] Task Completed
- Task: Corrective sprint against the independent Opus HOLD review of the V2 Service Desk redesign (1 P0, 3 P1, 6 smaller items). Orchestrator-implemented: Codex was unavailable for the whole session (ChatGPT-account usage limit, resets 2026-09-07), and the owner chose "implement it solo" over waiting.
- Commits on feature/v2-service-desk-redesign: f504016 (backend contract), 1a5cab4 (frontend workspace corrections), cc21b95 (light-mode tokenization), 5cce817 (escalated-close engine gate).
- P0 retry: TicketWorkspace showed TicketDebrief for any authoritative grade and the debrief had no way back, so a failed attempt 1 locked the student out permanently. TicketDebrief now offers "Start attempt N" whenever the attempt failed and the server still has an attempt left; TicketSessionProvider.startNextAttempt() posts to the existing POST /assignments/{id}/attempts and clears only that ticket's attempt-scoped client state (authoritative grade, debrief, ticket overlay, that scenario's remote-desktop progress). The server owns numbering and the ceiling; a 403 leaves the debrief up with an explanation. Attempt 1 keeps its grade as history.
- Escalation privacy: workspace_view.escalation is now {"available": true} for every scenario - no route, no rationale, no expected flag - and EscalateDialog/OutcomeBar no longer name a destination before the student chooses. The student picks reason AND destination from the shared ESCALATION_ROUTES list; the server decides correctness. stages[fix].mode stays the literal "fix" and the dead WorkflowRail escalate branch is deleted.
- Escalation grading: resolved = escalation_correct AND documentation_complete. escalation_correct stays true when only the note is missing so the debrief can say "right call, documentation still required".
- Debrief tiers: new coaching_tier. "limited" (failed with attempts left) withholds stronger_path, escalation_feedback and per-category specifics; "full" (passed, or out of attempts) reveals everything.
- Notes: ResolveDialog's own textarea is gone; it shows a read-only DocumentationSummary of the workspace note and closes with that exact note. ResolutionNotePanel is the only editable note field.
- Preview: ResolveDialog no longer predicts points or a pass - the browser cannot model escalation profiles or prohibited actions.
- Contract cleanup: dead resolve_blockers field removed (no consumer; emitted "complete_fix" for a ticket whose correct outcome was not to self-fix).
- Light mode: primitives, chrome, queue, workspace, embedded tools and modals all repaint from semantic tokens; the unreachable [data-theme='light'] .sd-* overrides (components layer always loses to a utility) are removed. The simulated Windows desktop deliberately stays light in both themes. Guarded by light-mode-coverage.test.tsx.
- Adjacent fix, flagged not silent: the browser's close gate required phases.fixed/verified for every phase-based ticket, so INC2506 could only be finished by performing the prohibited change - the new escalation grading was unreachable from the UI. An escalated ticket now closes without the local repair/verify gates but still requires the hand-off note.
- Verification: backend 877 pytest tests pass (full suite, 6m30s), compileall clean, ruff clean, alembic heads 0068_v2_runtime_stabilization with NO new migration. service-desk-app lint 5/5, typecheck 10/10, tests 405 (web 147 / simulation-engine 185 / shared 37 / ui 36) across 57 files, clean Next build.
- Limitations reported honestly: pnpm audit cannot reach the npm registry from this host (TLS socket error / timeout) - no dependency was added or changed in this sprint, so the dependency surface is identical to 649bb63. Browser E2E was not run (no disposable credentials in this environment). apps/web/app/layout.tsx still hardcodes data-theme="dark", so light mode is correct but not yet selectable by a student - recorded in the deferred backlog.
- INC2508 realism deliberately untouched: the generic containment event remains temporarily accepted, recorded as deferred item 9 in service-desk-app/docs/WORKSPACE.md.
- Production safety: no deploy, no production migration, no write to backend/nexus.db (every pytest/alembic invocation was scoped to a scratch DATABASE_URL), no service restart, no env change, not merged to main. Branch remains feature/v2-service-desk-redesign.
